&lt;project_context>
# 项目: schema-dag-extraction

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: cqa1-38874
- SSH 账户: root（调用 ssh_execute / submit_training_job 时传入 account="root"）
- SSH 连接命令: ssh connect.cqa1.seetacloud.com  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/schema-dag-extraction


## 环境搭建
连接服务器后先调 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果匹配，跳过基础环境搭建，只装项目特有依赖。已预装的包不要重复安装。

## 输出风格
回复简洁直接。只输出执行结果和必要信息，不要解释过程、不要客套、不要给未被要求的建议。
&lt;/project_context>
## 任务：诊断 fewrel_40rel_r2 OOM 崩溃 + 修复脚本

### 背景
exp_fewrel_40rel_r2 在 cqa1-38874 上跑 Qwen3-32B-FP8 时 OOM（vLLM EngineCore 初始化阶段 SIGKILL at shard 1/7 14%）。但同样的模型在 `run_fewrel_formal.py` 上成功跑完了 exp_fewrel_formal_80rel（完整 80-rel 实验）。

exp_fewrel_40rel_r3 目前标记为 RUNNING（PID 300803），但可能已经完成或失败。请一并检查。

### 服务器信息
- 服务器: cqa1-38874, account: root
- Python 环境: conda base (`source /root/miniconda3/etc/profile.d/conda.sh && conda activate base`)
- 项目路径: /root/schema-dag-extraction
- GPU: cuda:0, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell 98GB

### 步骤

1. **检查 r3 状态**：
   ```bash
   ps -p 300803 -o pid,stat,etime,cmd 2>/dev/null || echo "PID_DEAD"
   cat ~/runs/schema-dag-extraction/exp_fewrel_40rel_r3.log | tail -50
   ```
   确认 r3 是否还在跑、是否成功、是否也 OOM。

2. **比较 vLLM 参数**：读取以下两个文件，逐项对比 vLLM 相关参数：
   - `src/run_fewrel_40rel.py`（失败的）
   - `src/run_fewrel_formal.py`（成功的）
   
   重点对比：
   - `gpu_memory_utilization`
   - `enforce_eager`
   - `max_model_len`
   - `dtype`
   - `tensor_parallel_size`
   - `max_num_seqs`
   - `max_num_batched_tokens`
   - 其他可能影响显存的参数

3. **找出差异**：
   - 列出所有不同的参数及其值
   - 判断哪个参数导致了 OOM
   - 如果参数完全一样，检查是否有其他因素（如 batch 大小、prompt 长度、并发请求数）

4. **修复 run_fewrel_40rel.py**：
   - 将 vLLM 参数对齐到成功配置
   - 如果参数已经一样，降 `gpu_memory_utilization` 到 0.85
   - 确保 `FLASHINFER_DISABLE_VERSION_CHECK=1` 环境变量存在

5. **检查/修复启动脚本**：
   - 检查 `scripts/run_fewrel_40rel.sh`（如果存在）
   - 确保环境变量、路径都正确

6. **验证修复**（不要实际跑 GPU，只做语法检查）：
   ```bash
   python3 -c "import ast; ast.parse(open('src/run_fewrel_40rel.py').read()); print('SYNTAX_OK')"
   ```

### 产出
- 详细的参数对比表（run_fewrel_40rel.py vs run_fewrel_formal.py）
- 修改后的 run_fewrel_40rel.py（已对齐参数）
- exp_fewrel_40rel_r3 的当前状态（还在跑/成功/失败）
- 如果 r3 已完成，报告其结果

### 注意事项
- 这是 CPU 诊断任务，不要启动 GPU 进程
- 用 ssh_read_only 读文件和查状态，ssh_execute 只用于修改文件
- 不要创建 venv，用 conda base

batches per count,\\n    count_1 = 80 solo batches (one per relation type).\\n    1000 samples balanced across active relations.\\n\\nexp2 (BCS controlled): count={8,16}, 1 low-BCS + 1 high-BCS + 25 random = 27 per count.\\n    Same 1000 samples.\\n\\nMetric: accuracy (RE is single-label classification per sample).\\n\\nUsage:\\n    # Auto two-stage (recommended — avoids vLLM fork deadlock):\\n    python3 src/run_fewrel_formal.py --mode exp1 --use_all\\n\\n    # Manual two-stage:\\n    python3 src/run_fewrel_formal.py --stage preprocess --mode exp1 --use_all\\n    python3 src/run_fewrel_formal.py --stage extract --mode exp1\\n\\n    # Dry run:\\n    python3 src/run_fewrel_formal.py --mode exp1 --use_all --dry_run\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport Path(\\\"/root/schema-dag-extraction\\\")\\n\\nEXP1_COUNTS = [1, 4, 8, 16, 32, 40]\\nEXP2_COUNTS = [8, 16]\\nEXP1_RANDOM_PER_COUNT = 5\\nEXP2_RANDOM_PER_COUNT = 25\\nDEFAULT_N_SAMPLES = 1000\\n\\n\\ndef evaluate_re(results):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Per-batch accuracy and per-type accuracy breakdown.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    batch_stats = defaultdict(lambda: {\\\"correct\\\": 0, \\\"total\\\": 0, r[\\\"batch_types\\\"]\\n        gold = r[\\\"ground_truth\\\"]\\n        pred = r[\\\"prediction\\\"]\\n        s[\\\"per_type\\\"][gold][\\\"total\\\"] += 1\\n        if pred == gold:\\n            s[\\\"correct\\\"] += 1\\n            s[\\\"per_type\\\"][gold][\\\"correct\\\"] += 1\\n\\n    out = {}\\n    for bid, s in batch_stats.items():\\n        per_type_acc = {}\\n        for t, ts in s[\\\"per_type\\\"].items():\\n            per_type_acc[t] = {\\n                \\\"accuracy\\\": ts[\\\"correct\\\"] / ts[\\\"total\\\"] if ts[\\\"total\\\"] else 0.0,\\n                \\\"correct\\\": ts[\\\"correct\\\"], \\\"total\\\": ts[\\\"total\\\"],\\n            }\\n        out[bid] = {\\n            \\\"accuracy\\\": s[\\\"correct\\\"] / s[\\\"total\\\"] if s[\\\"total\\\"] samples, batch_config, relation_defs, max_model_len=4096, output_dir=None):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Run RE extraction using vLLM with constrained JSON decoding.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n    sys.path.insert(0, <details><summary>Logged reasoning block</summary>

```text
Now I have more clarity:

1. **r3 status**: Completed successfully (EXIT_CODE=0). The log shows only the last few lines - exp1 done, starting exp2, then ALL DONE.

2. **r2 crash**: The log clearly shows OOM at shard 1/7 (14%) during model loading. The EngineCore process was SIGKILL'd. The crash happened in `run_fewrel_40rel.py` line 88 where `LLM()` is initialized.

3. **r3 log head = r3 log tail**: It seems the r3 log is very short (only the few lines we saw). 

Looking at the r3 log more carefully, it appears exp1 was already completed before r3 started, then it ran exp2's preprocessing steps—loading the FewRel data, processing the 80 relation types, sampling 960 examples, and computing embeddings with CPU fallback—before finishing cleanly with EXIT_CODE=0.

Now I need to focus on the actual comparison between the two Python files. The vLLM parameters in the `run_re_extraction` function are identical across both versions: `gpu_memory_utilization=0.95`, `enforce_eager=True`, `dtype="bfloat16"`, `max_model_len=4096`, `trust_remote_code=True`, and `disable_log_stats=True`. So the OOM error wasn't triggered by different vLLM configuration settings.

Looking at the actual vLLM engine output from the r2 crash log, I can see the non-default arguments that were being used when it failed.

The crash happened at shard 1/7 (14%) during weight loading for the Qwen3-32B-FP8 model on a single GPU with 98GB of memory. With `gpu_memory_utilization=0.95`, that should allocate about 93GB for the model, which should be sufficient for a ~32GB model. The `tensor_parallel_size` isn't explicitly set in either file, so it defaults to 1. The OOM is happening during the weight loading phase, not during inference, which narrows down the possible causes.

I'm wondering if another process was using the GPU at the same time during r2, causing the OOM. That would explain why r3 succeeded — the GPU was free by then. Looking at the logs more carefully, r2 crashed during exp1 extraction, while r3 shows exp1 was already marked as done and started exp2 preprocessing instead. But then r3 says "ALL DONE" without actually running the GPU extraction for exp2, which is odd. Let me look at the r3 log more carefully to understand what actually happened.

The r3 log shows it loaded the FewRel data, sampled 960 examples, computed MiniLM embeddings on CPU, and then exited successfully — but there's no vLLM extraction phase for exp2 shown. Either exp2 extraction was already completed, or it ran as a subprocess, or there's a bug that skipped it. I should check what script launched r3 and whether exp2 results actually exist. Also, I'm noticing that r2 used a relative path for the output directory (`artifacts/fewrel_40rel`), which could be relevant to understanding what happened.

The core issue with r2 is that it crashed while loading model weights at shard 1/7 — very early in the process. With 98GB of VRAM available, a 32GB model should load without problems, so something else was likely consuming GPU memory at that moment. I need to examine the actual differences between the scripts more carefully and check what shell command launched both r2 and r3.

The `=== ALL DONE ===` came right after preprocessing with `EXIT_CODE=0`. This means exp2 was likely run as preprocess-only and the wrapper didn't launch GPU extraction.

Wait, let me think again. Looking at the r3 log:
```
=== exp1 DONE, starting exp2 ===


...Loading FewRel data...
...computing MiniLM embeddings (CPU fallback)
=== ALL DONE ===
EXIT_CODE=0
```

This is the output of a shell script wrapper that runs exp1, then exp2. exp1 was already done (from a previous run), so it went straight to exp2. exp2 preprocessing completed and then the extraction subprocess either succeeded or wasn't launched. But there are no extraction logs in the log file, and the artifacts dir only has `preprocess_exp1.json` and `batch_config_exp1.json` — no `predictions_*.json` or `eval_*.json`. So the extraction phase never actually ran or produced output.

The shell script says "ALL DONE" but neither experiment has generated actual results yet — just preprocessing files. Let me check the shell script itself to understand the flow, and also look at the truncated middle section of the 40rel.py file around lines 88-100 to see what's being cut off.

The r2 log shows the EngineCore subprocess getting killed by OOM while loading model weights at 14% completion, which is strange since a 32B FP8 model should fit comfortably on a 98GB GPU. I should check if another process was using GPU memory at the time, or if the two experiment files are actually identical except for minor differences. Since the vLLM parameters are identical between the two versions, the OOM issue likely stems from external factors like concurrent GPU processes or system issues. Following the task instructions, I should lower `gpu_memory_utilization` to 0.85 as a safeguard. Let me check the shell script and current GPU usage to understand the setup better.

Now I have the full picture. Let me also see the rest of the file (lines 200-388) to catch any differences I might have missed.

But first, let me compile what I know:

**Key findings:**

1. **r3 status**: Completed with EXIT_CODE=0, but the log is only 9 lines. It only did preprocessing (no GPU extraction). The `=== exp1 DONE, starting exp2 ===` message suggests the wrapper script detected exp1 results already exist and skipped to exp2. But exp2 then only did preprocessing and ended with "ALL DONE". 

The artifacts directory confirms this: only `preprocess_exp1.json`, `batch_config_exp1.json`, and `relation_sim_matrix.pt` — no predictions. Looking more carefully at the r3 log, the wrapper script prints `=== exp1 DONE, starting exp2 ===` and then runs the exp2 Python script which does preprocessing before ending. Let me trace through the shell script logic to understand what's actually happening here.

The shell script shows two separate Python calls with different modes — exp1 for the count curve and exp2 for BCS-controlled — but r3's log starts with a different message that doesn't match the echo statements in the script. This suggests r3 might have been run with a different wrapper script entirely, not the one I'm looking at.

Looking at r3's log more carefully, it says `=== exp1 DONE, starting exp2 ===`, which indicates the wrapper detected that exp1 had already completed. But r2 only finished preprocessing, not the actual extraction step that produces predictions. The wrapper is probably checking for the existence of a preprocessing file rather than verifying that predictions were actually generated, so it incorrectly assumed exp1 was done.

The critical issue is that neither r2 nor r3 actually produced any predictions — they both stopped before that final step. Now I need to look at what the vLLM parameters were set to.

Checking the vLLM configuration across both run_fewrel_40rel.py and run_fewrel_formal.py, they're using identical settings: 0.95 GPU memory utilization, eager execution mode, bfloat16 precision, a 4096 token limit, and remote code trust enabled, with no tensor parallelism or batching constraints specified. Since the formal run succeeded with these exact same parameters, the OOM during r2 was likely caused by either another GPU process running in the background or the two-stage subprocess architecture where the main process was holding onto data in memory while the extraction subprocess ran.

Actually, I need to reconsider this more carefully. In `--stage all` mode, the parent process loads 56,000 FewRel samples, the MiniLM embedding model, and a similarity matrix, then forks a subprocess for extraction. When the child process starts, it initially shares the parent's memory through copy-on-write, but then imports vLLM and loads the full LLM model while the parent is still running and waiting for the subprocess to complete. The combined memory footprint of the parent's ~1-2GB of data plus the MiniLM model alongside the child's vLLM model loading could be what triggered the OOM.

Wait, but `subprocess.run` actually spawns a completely new Python process rather than forking, so the parent's memory shouldn't directly impact the child's GPU memory allocation. The SIGKILL happening at 14% of the 31.97 GiB checkpoint (around 4.5 GiB loaded) is odd given that with `gpu_memory_utilization=0.95` on a 98GB GPU there should be roughly 93GB available. The most plausible explanation is that another GPU process was consuming memory when the second run started, whereas the first successful run had exclusive GPU access. Since the parameters are identical between runs, I should reduce `gpu_memory_utilization` to 0.85 to provide more headroom.

Looking at the timestamps more carefully, the second run crashed around 00:52:09 while loading weights, and the third run started about 5 minutes later at 00:57:38. Both were launched through the same nohup wrapper, which likely had `set -eo pipefail` configured, meaning the shell script would have exited after the second run's failure. But the third run has a different startup message, suggesting it was launched by a separate wrapper instance.

The key issue is that the second run's failure should have prevented the script from proceeding to the second experiment, yet the third run's log shows `=== exp1 DONE, starting exp2 ===`, which isn't coming from the main shell script. This suggests the third run was launched by a different script entirely.

Rather than trace the exact launch mechanism, I should focus on the actual fix: reducing `gpu_memory_utilization` from 0.95 to 0.85 in the Python config, confirming the environment variable is already set in the shell script, and checking the syntax. I need to see the rest of the Python file to make sure there aren't other relevant settings I'm missing.

Looking at the two scripts side by side, the differences are intentional—the experiment counts differ for 40-rel versus 80-rel, the output directories are appropriately named, and the 40-rel version has some incremental saving improvements. The vLLM constructor calls are identical, so the OOM wasn't caused by the script configuration itself. It was likely an environmental issue or transient GPU memory pressure, which lowering the utilization threshold should help mitigate.

_source line 60 · 2026-05-15T17:09:57.809Z_

完整分析完成。结论：

## 诊断结果

### exp_fewrel_40rel_r3 状态
- PID 300803 已死（日志仅 9 行）
- r3 **只完成了 exp2 预处理**，未执行 GPU extraction
- artifacts 目录只有 `preprocess_exp1.json`、`batch_config_exp1.json`、`relation_sim_matrix.pt` — 无预测结果
- **exp1 和 exp2 均未产出实际预测**

### r2 OOM 根因
- SIGKILL 发生在 EngineCore 加载 safetensors shard 1/7 (14%) 阶段
- 模型大小 31.97 GiB，GPU 98GB，`gpu_memory_utilization=0.95` 应该足够

### 参数对比表

| 参数 | run_fewrel_40rel.py | run_fewrel_formal.py | 差异 |
|------|---------------------|---------------------|------|
| `gpu_memory_utilization` | 0.95 | 0.95 | **相同** |
| `enforce_eager` | True | True | 相同 |
| `max_model_len` | 4096 | 4096 | 相同 |
| `dtype` | "bfloat16" | "bfloat16" | 相同 |
| `trust_remote_code` | True | True | 相同 |
| `disable_log_stats` | True | True | 相同 |
| `tensor_parallel_size` | 未设置(默认1) | 未设置(默认1) | 相同 |
| `max_num_seqs` | 未设置 | 未设置 | 相同 |
| `max_num_batched_tokens` | 未设置 | 未设置 | 相同 |
| `EXP1_COUNTS` | [1,4,8,16,32,40] | [1,4,8,16,32,64,80] | 设计差异 |
| `output_dir` | fewrel_40rel | fewrel_formal | 设计差异 |
| incremental save | 有 | 无 | 40rel增强 |

**vLLM 参数完全一致。** r2 OOM 最可能是当时有其他 GPU 进程占用显存。按指示降 `gpu_memory_utilization` 到 0.85 作为安全裕量。

修改 `run_fewrel_40rel.py`：

### exp_fewrel_40rel_r3
- 已结束，EXIT_CODE=0，但**只做了 exp2 预处理**

### r2 OOM 根因
- vLLM 参数与成功的 `run_fewrel_formal.py` **完全一致**
- OOM 发生在模型权重加载阶段（shard 1/7），不是推理阶段
